But what thousands of Amazon shoppers appreciate most is that it works for both the brows and face. Plus, the vibrant shades also make the tool easy to spot in your makeup bag. No wonder Schick is the No. 1 best seller on Amazon. It’s affordable and effective, and it has amassed 152,600 perfect 5-star Amazon reviews.

Truly, the only downside is mastering your dermaplaning technique. Some reviewers accidentally nicked their face by applying too much pressure. To avoid this, pull your face taut, slowly glide the blade across the skin, and don’t shave over the same spot twice.

Doing treatments at home (versus in-office) and the results they bring are slightly trickier to predict and come with more risks, especially the chance of infections, scarring, or skin damage, cautions Dr. Shirazi. Her advice is to take it slow and try it only if you're comfortable. It's important to thoroughly cleanse the area you'll be treating, clean your hands, sanitize the tools, and tidy up the space, she says. "Be informed about dermaplaning—the more you know, the better you can assess the risks."

Keep in mind that dermaplaning might not suit those with sensitive or acne-prone skin. "You need a strong skin barrier to tolerate the treatment, so if you have dry skin or flaking red skin, it’s best to let the skin heal before dermaplaning," she says. "It can also worsen certain skin conditions like acne, psoriasis, eczema, and hives."

The number one no-no when it comes to dermaplaning: Don't over-exfoliate. "If you are on retinoids or exfoliants like glycolic or salicylic acid, you need to stop them a week before," says Dr. Shirazi. She also warns against dermaplaning if you have active wounds or skin conditions such as eczema and active wounds, as your skin needs to heal fully before dermaplaning. Lastly, don’t forget to thoroughly cleanse both the skin and hands before using the tool.

If you don't know how to use an at-home dermaplaning device, follow these simple steps with your manual or electric tool: Place the device at a 90-degree angle on the skin, starting at the top of the cheekbone near the hairline. Hold the skin taut as you gently glide the tool toward your nose. Continue until you have covered the entire cheek and jaw area. Repeat these steps across the forehead, lip, and chin.

Dermaplaning can be performed once every four to eight weeks, according to Dr. Shirazi. However, some brands recommend using their specific tools more often. Make sure to read the label on your device or consult your dermatologist if you're unsure how often to use your tool.

#4977. US Bank Regulator Issues First AI Framework as Banks Battle Rogue Agents

US Bank Regulator Issues First AI Framework as Banks Battle Rogue Agents

The U.S. Office of the Comptroller of the Currency issued its first supervisory framework for artificial intelligence in banking today, telling examiners to focus less on how powerful an AI system is and more on what happens when it makes a mistake.

The OCC's framework addresses that very concern, although the regulator has not linked its issuance to those specific incidents.

In a section covering operational resilience, the agency warns that external agents operated by customers, fintechs, or other businesses could create risks for banks even when the banks don't control those systems.

It suggests examiners consider measures including identity verification, delegated permissions, transaction limits, monitoring, and the ability to suspend or revoke access.

The guidelines aim to tackle the dilemma of how to let customers use AI assistants to manage their finances without giving autonomous software unrestricted access to accounts that banks are already facing.

The new OCC guidance arrives less than two weeks after reports that major banks were discovering unauthorized third-party AI agents operating inside customer accounts, sometimes taking actions that appeared to go beyond users' instructions.

Morgan Stanley was blocking outside agents, while JPMorgan, Bank of America, and Citi were restricting access, according to an Axios report on October 22. Bank executives described the situation as "horrifying," raising questions about who controls an autonomous agent once it has access to someone's money.

The OCC's framework makes a distinction between an AI system's technical sophistication and the damage it could cause.

A simple agent authorized to initiate financial transactions may present greater risk than a more advanced model limited to writing internal documents, the regulator said.

Under the 22-page framework, examiners can consider the consequences of failure, whether an action can be reversed, and how much control humans retain over the system.

The agency outlines four illustrative risk tiers. Fully autonomous payment agents and real-time credit decision systems fall into the highest category, while basic internal productivity tools occupy the lowest.

Higher-risk systems may warrant tighter restrictions on autonomy, independent testing, and human approval before consequential actions can proceed.

The framework also addresses AI systems that continue operating while making harmful decisions. Suggested safeguards include circuit breakers, rate limits, restricted permissions, and emergency shutdown controls.

Those considerations are especially relevant to the outside agents described in Axios's earlier reporting, where banks struggled to distinguish legitimate customer-authorized software from systems operating beyond their intended permissions.

The OCC stressed that the document does not create new regulatory requirements or enforceable standards for banks.

Instead, it gives examiners a way to evaluate AI within existing operational, compliance, and cybersecurity risk categories. Banks are not required to establish a separate AI risk category.

The document uses the mandatory term "Super Intelligence," which the Donald Trump administration insists on using in place of “artificial intelligence,” but its scope includes conventional AI and “autonomous agents” rather than only hypothetical systems with intelligence beyond human capabilities.

The framework covers AI used in payments, fraud detection, customer service, and other banking operations. It also extends its risk considerations to external AI agents connecting through bank channels.

The OCC said it intends to update the guidance as the technology develops. For now, its illustrative risk scale puts autonomous payment agents in the highest-risk category, regardless of how well they perform in testing.

#4972. Coinbase Says 99% of Customer Crypto Is Offline and Out of Hackers’ Reach

Coinbase Says 99% of Customer Crypto Is Offline and Out of Hackers’ Reach

Coinbase is moving to reassure customers that what happened at Binance can’t easily happen there.

In a blog post Wednesday, the U.S. exchange said it wasn’t affected by last week’s hack of Binance, in which attackers drained about $6.4 billion from the rival exchange’s hot wallets.

Coinbase said it keeps more than 99% of customer assets in cold storage, meaning offline wallets that can’t be reached over the internet. It moves funds into internet-connected hot wallets only as customers need them.

The Binance incident “has understandably left customers asking” how Coinbase guards against a similar attack, the post said.

Coinbase laid out several layers of protection. Private keys for its cold wallets are split into pieces and never fully reassembled, even when signing transactions, and no single employee has standing access to the hot-wallet signing service. Strict limits cap how fast funds can move from cold to hot wallets, and unusual withdrawal spikes are held until staff review them. The exchange can also trigger “kill switches” that take signing keys offline entirely.

The company said it assumes employees will be targeted by phishing, so its controls are designed so that a stolen password, session or device can’t be used on its own, and sensitive actions require multiple approvals.

That focus on insiders carries weight for Coinbase. In May 2025, it disclosed that criminals had bribed overseas customer support agents to steal personal data on less than 1% of its monthly transacting users, then demanded a $20 million ransom that Coinbase refused to pay.

Last week, Coinbase said a security incident at analytics vendor Amplitude exposed contact data for a “small subset” of customers, though funds weren’t affected.

Coinbase also said customer assets are held 1:1 and aren’t lent out unless customers direct it to, and it reported $8.6 billion in cash and cash equivalents and $377 billion in assets on its platform as of Sept. 30. It flagged artificial intelligence as a fast-changing threat and pledged further security investment.

The Binance theft, which the FBI has attributed to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group, has rattled the industry. Blockchain analytics firm Elliptic found that the laundering spanned about 12,000 wallets in under four days, while stablecoin issuers froze about 44% of the haul.

Separately, Brazil’s Mercado Bitcoin halted withdrawals after a $40 million hack, and French bank Crédit Mutuel Arkéa recently confirmed a cyberattack that led to a $133 million crypto theft.

#4970. Trump-linked tokens lead crypto losses after Democrats sweep in the US midterms

The official Trump and World Liberty Financial tokens were the two biggest losers among the top 100 cryptocurrencies on Wednesday, even as Bitcoin and Ether rallied.

Two crypto tokens linked to US President Donald Trump led losses among the top 100 digital assets on Wednesday following the US midterm elections, raising questions about their future as the broader crypto market rallied.

Official Trump (TRUMP) fell 17% over 24 hours to $1.33, while World Liberty Financial (WLFI) dropped over 7% to $0.047, according to CoinMarketCap data from around 5:32 pm UTC. TRUMP is the president’s official memecoin, launched in January 2025, while WLFI is the governance token of World Liberty Financial, a Trump family-backed decentralized finance (DeFi) project launched in 2024.

The declines followed a wave of Democratic victories, including surprise wins in Texas, in Tuesday’s midterm elections, with the party winning control of both the House and Senate from Republicans.

The results could not only make it harder for Trump to push forward his crypto plans but also bring more scrutiny to his family’s crypto businesses. Analysts say the losses also raise questions about whether political sentiment alone can fuel demand for Trump-linked tokens.

Bitcoin (BTC) rose more than 4% over 24 hours to around $80,500, while Ether (ETH) climbed 6% to $2,433, according to CoinMarketCap data. Other major cryptocurrencies also posted gains, with BNB rising 3%, XRP climbing 2.6% and Solana (SOL) gaining 2.5%. The overall crypto market rose 3.4% to $2.69 trillion.

Meanwhile, the losses extended to other Trump family-linked tokens, such as the MELANIA memecoin, associated with First Lady Melania Trump. MELANIA dropped nearly 13% over 24 hours to around $0.066.

A separate token named after Vice President JD Vance (VANCE) also dropped nearly 12%, although it has no confirmed official connection to the vice president.

The losses were further reflected across the broader political memecoin market, with the sector’s total market capitalization falling more than 16% on the day to $477 million. Trading volume jumped 123% to $437 million, per CoinMarketCap data.

Related: US midterm elections 2026 as it happened: Democrats win control of Congress

Benjamin Sarquis Peillard, founder and CEO of Cap, a DeFi platform focused on stablecoin yields, told Cointelegraph that investors appear to be treating politically linked tokens differently from other cryptocurrencies.

“Bitcoin and other established assets respond to a combination of liquidity, institutional flows and macroeconomic conditions, while politically linked tokens can be more sensitive to shifts in expectations around Washington,” he said. “Even as the broader market recovers, those tokens may face selling pressure if investors begin reassessing their risk exposure.”

And while the selloff could be indicative of a short-term reaction to the election results, Peillard said the “longer-term question is what supports demand for these tokens beyond their association with a political figure.”

Beyond the market reaction, the election results also raise questions about the future of US crypto legislation, including the currently stalled Digital Asset Market Clarity Act, commonly referred to as the CLARITY Act.

The bill, which aims to establish a regulatory framework for digital assets, failed to advance in the Senate earlier this year after falling short of the 60 votes required. Democrats were pushing for stronger ethical provisions and safeguards against conflicts of interest involving Trump’s crypto ventures — four Republicans also opposed advancing the legislation.

“The midterm results introduce a new layer of regulatory uncertainty, particularly around the pace and direction of digital asset legislation,” Peillard said. “For politically linked tokens, that uncertainty can have a more immediate impact because investor sentiment is partly tied to expectations around the political environment.”

While the crypto sector continues to have support — and opposition — from lawmakers in both parties, it remains to be seen how the election results will affect crypto legislation.

Peillard said that for the broader industry, the “more important question is whether Congress can establish a durable regulatory framework that provides clarity regardless of which party holds power.”

#4969. Do Your Muscles Need a ‘Creatine Washout?’ It Depends.

The benefits might be more mental than physical.

CREATINE IS THE prom king of social media—everyone’s talking about it. And not always nice things, either. Because for all the (very warranted) complements about how creatine can help you grow stronger, there’s also some (baseless) behind-the-back whispers about how it can make your hair fall out, poison you with heavy metals, and yes even kill you.

Between this polarization, there’s the seemingly reasonable personal stance of Andrew Huberman, PhD: Take creatine, but also take a creatine break.

Just in case you’re unfamiliar, a Huberman is a neuroscientist and associate professor in the Departments of Neurobiology and Ophthalmology at Stanford University School of Medicine. And he only hosts one of the most popular health podcasts of all time, Huberman Lab. When Huberman says anything about anything, people listen.

So when Huberman posted that he does a “creatine washout” every four months “just to see what effect it continues to have over time,” you have to wonder.

IT’S THE TIME period after you stop supplementing with creatine. The term is used frequently in scientific research that assesses the effects of creatine supplementation, often in athletic performance studies.

But, admittedly, there aren’t that many studies that look at the creatine washout phase itself. The most recent goes all the way back to 2004: “Effects of repeated creatine supplementation on muscle, plasma, and urine creative levels.”

In that study researchers found that levels of muscle phosphocreatine—that’s the amount of creatine stored within muscle cells—did not return to pre-supplementation baselines even after a 30-day washout period.

Since that 2004 study, there hasn’t been any supportive—or contradictory—science, but the lead author says the findings hold up more than 20 years later.

“Four to six weeks is a typical response and depends on dietary creatine, response to supplement, etc.,” says Eric Rawson, PhD, FACSM, Chair of the Department of Health, Nutrition, and Exercise Science at Messiah University, and a co-author of the 2004 washout study.

MAYBE, BUT NOT for reasons you might think.

“Questions about creatine washout and/or cycling supplementation are best handled from both physiological and behavioral perspectives,” says Rawson. “When I am asked about washout and cycling, I always ask people if they have similar concerns about their multivitamin. Or their calcium supplements. Or whey protein.”

Meaning, do you have the same washout concerns about creatine as you do your other supplements? Probably not.

“There appears to be a lingering concern about long-term creatine supplementation that is not supported by data.”

That said, Rawson says that some people, including many athletes, can feel overwhelmed by demands of maintaining their fitness edge—to the point where it’s good to take a break for mental health.

“Taking an off-season break from some of the usual routines, which includes, training, meal frequency, macro-nutrient intake goals, and the daily dietary supplement routine, can provide a break and perhaps some relief from the usual routine,” he says.

How long a break is sort of up to you and your long-term goals. But let your body and brain be the judge of that—not whatever you hear on social.

Paul Kita is a Deputy Editor at Men's Health, where he has covered food, cooking, nutrition, supplements, grooming, tech, travel, and fatherhood at the brand for more than 15 years. He is also the author of two Men's Health cookbooks, Guy Gourmet and A Man, A Pan, A Plan, and the winner of a James Beard Award.

#4968. Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google is giving schools more freedom to reject artificial intelligence—provided they're also willing to give up Google Search, which is arguably what everybody in the world uses Google for.

The company announced Tuesday that its AI products will be disabled by default for K-12 student accounts unless administrators explicitly enable them. But schools that want to ban AI entirely will also have to disable Google's search engine, a tool millions of students rely on for everyday research.

Chris Phillips, Google's vice president and general manager of education, tried to justify the reasoning during a meeting with educators.

“Search and AI are deeply integrated,” he said according to Education Week. Schools uncomfortable with AI can choose not to enable Search at all.

Google says administrators will be able to activate or disable Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom, and Search individually. That means schools can block the standalone chatbots while keeping Search available, but they cannot remove AI from Search itself.

For teachers who want students to research sources without receiving AI-generated answers, the company is effectively offering two options: accept its AI-powered search or find another search engine.

The announcement is already drawing accusations that Google is using its dominance in education to push a technology many schools are still trying to understand, regulate or restrict.

Google says the changes are intended to address concerns that AI chatbots can undermine learning by giving students ready-made answers instead of encouraging them to solve problems themselves.

Beginning in early 2027, schools that enable Search will be offered versions with conventional AI features, such as AI Overviews and AI Mode, or the education-focused Guided Learning experience, according to The New York Times.

Neither option provides the traditional search experience without generative AI.

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani accused the company of “trying to use its monopoly power to force AI into our public schools,” according to CBS News.

Former Federal Trade Commission chair Lina Khan was equally critical, accusing Google of using its search monopoly to coerce schools into adopting AI.

The criticism comes as some of America's largest school districts are actively restricting generative AI. New York City, for example, imposed a one-year moratorium on student-facing generative AI through eighth grade, covering nearly 600,000 students, while Los Angeles has also restricted the technology.

Los Angeles officials had specifically hoped to preserve Google Search while removing AI-generated summaries. Under the newly announced controls, that won't be an option.

Google's position also arrives amid continuing antitrust scrutiny. A federal court ruled in 2024 that Google unlawfully monopolized the U.S. search market, and the company has faced separate legal challenges over its decision to integrate AI summaries into search results.

School administrators are expected to receive the new controls in early 2027. They will be able to decide which AI products students can access, and even apply different settings by grade level. A Google Search option without AI, however, is not among the choices.

That said, for schools that want to, ahem, unofficially ban AI without losing Google search, our guide may provide a quick solution. It takes 5 minutes and lasts forever.

#4966. I’m a Doctor and I Won’t Touch Honey Packs. Here’s What I Do Before Sex Instead

Ahead, three tried-and-true strategies for better, longer-lasting erections.

IF YOU HAVE difficulty getting erections or simply want to spruce up your sex life, chances are you’ve heard about honey packs. The sachets of the sweet stuff are all over social media, promising enhanced sexual performance through stronger, long-lasting erections.

At first glance, you might find this a “natural” alternative for young guys who don’t want pills or painful-sounding ED procedures like shock wave therapy. After all, the packets do feature pictures of honey and bees swarming on honeycombs, and honey is usually one of the first ingredients—a food many dudes already drizzle in oatmeal and tea. What could go wrong?

MH did a deep dive last year into whether honey packs actually work. The TL;DR version is that despite the alluring promise of near-instant, rock-hard erections, you’re really taking a gamble on what you’re putting in your body. Natural ingredients like honey and ginseng provide a “trivial” performance-enhancing effect and multiple honey pack supplement companies have been flagged by the FDA for having hidden and unknown dosages of ingredients not included in the label, like tadalafil (the active ingredient in Cialis), sildenafil (the active ingredient in Viagra), and acetaminophen.

That’s why doctors have big safety concerns about honey packs, says Scott Miller, MD, a urologist and director of robotic surgery at Wellstar Health System. “If they can’t tell me what’s in them, then it’s an absolute no.” So if honey packs are out, what are some ways to help you get hard and stay hard? We turned to the experts, asking urologists what they do to prepare in lieu of popping a pack.

HONEY PACKS (AND even gas station dick pills) aren’t worth your time or money. But not all supplements need to be ruled out, says Philip Werthman, MD, a urologist and director of the Center for Male Reproductive Medicine and Vasectomy Reversal. For guys with mild ED or who just want to optimize their libido, he recommends L-arginine supplements.

L-arginine is an amino acid that supports blood flow through nitric oxide production. Nitric oxide allows blood vessels to open fully and allow more blood flow to come into the penis.

“The penis is a hemodynamic organ. It works on blood flow. There has to be stimulation and a relationship that is functional between the nerves and the blood vessels,” says Dr. Werthman. “Anything that helps nitric oxide stick around longer will cause more blood flow to come in and maybe give a better quality erection.”

If possible, he recommends choosing L-arginine supplements containing L-citrulline (a precursor to L-arginine) or pycnogenol. The latter is an antioxidant made from the bark of a tree and is a powerful antioxidant used often in the fertility world, says Dr. Werthman. In concert with L-arginine, the combo helps to boost erections.

There is one caveat. For the effects to really work, you need to take this supplement on a daily basis. A single scoop the night before a sexual encounter won’t make much of a difference.

“It’s the kind of thing you put into your supplement stack,” says Dr. Werthman. Beyond healthy erections, nitric oxide helps with increasing blood flow all over the body, potentially providing extra benefits to your brain, heart, and muscles if you’re working out.

Buyer Beware: Supplements are not FDA-regulated, and some can get sketchy. Consider getting a product tested and certified by a third-party testing facility to verify the supplement’s safety and quality.

OYSTERS AND CHAMPAGNE get a lot of hype as aphrodisiacs. But Dr. Werthman says the true value is eating foods high in L-arginine. Here are some examples:

Additionally, watermelon is one of the best foods to eat on the day you’re having sex because of its high citrulline content, says Dr. Werthman. So, while spontaneous sex is great, scheduling sex can help you fuel your body (and boner) accordingly.

As for what’s on the don’t eat list? Nothing, but both urologists say it’s best to avoid alcohol before sex. You might gain a little liquid courage, but the buzz kills erections. Alcohol slows down brain signals related to arousal and decreases the ability of blood vessels to trap blood in the penis.

CORTISOL, ALSO KNOWN as your body’s stress hormone, is one of the biggest killers of testosterone and erections. When your body is in fight-or-flight mode, Dr. Werthman says, the last thing it’s thinking about is having sex.

The trademarks of a stressful lifestyle—lack of sleep, long working hours, and a poor diet—can all create barriers to intimacy, says Dr. Miller. Setting aside time to take care of yourself physically will help with performing at your best.

For example, a meditation session or some light stretching can relieve some stress temporarily. You also might want to consider a quick workout. Now, Dr. Werthman warns that a single sweat session won’t do much for your penile health (over time, consistent exercise does support blood flow and cardiovascular health, helping you last longer). But in terms of mental health, the stress-relieving benefits of pushups or a quick jog can help you get amped for the main event. Plus, the endorphins in your body can shake off any pre-sex nerves and make you feel more confident in your body. One study involving over 128,000 people found all types of physical activity—walking, resistance training, Pilates, and yoga—effective in improving anxiety symptoms.

Granted, for some people, sex itself is the stress reliever. But if you tense up thinking about the actual act, Dr. Werthman advises taking it slow. Get out of your head and focus on increasing erotic arousal through kissing and other forms of foreplay, then seeing if you want to go further. “It’s about being comfortable with your partner,” he says. “You want a dynamic where if you get a fail-to-launch situation, they understand. Because it happens—not every encounter leads to a touchdown.”

#4963. ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

The internet may soon have an unlimited supply of AI-generated dancing cats, courtesy of ChatGPT.

OpenAI is rolling out a feature that lets users generate animated GIFs directly inside ChatGPT, according to The Verge. The feature reportedly comes powered by its new image-generation model called Images 2.6, which OpenAI has yet to formally introduce through its usual product announcements.

Users can describe an animation in plain English or upload an existing photo and ask ChatGPT to bring it to life. Once the GIF is generated, they can adjust its playback speed, inspect individual frames, make edits, and share the finished animation.

OpenAI demonstrated the capability with a simple request: “can you make a gif of a cat dancing?” The resulting interface reportedly includes controls to change animation speed and examine the GIF frame by frame.

We tried generating GIFs using ChatGPT, but the feature was not available on our accounts at the time of testing. In our interaction, the model generates a series of frames, but not the animation.

The rollout appears to be gradual, so users may have to keep asking for their dancing cats a little longer.

The GIF maker is reportedly part of a broader Images 2.6 update that promises faster image generation. The Verge reports that OpenAI is bringing the model to ChatGPT and Codex users across web, desktop, and mobile platforms.

Developers are also getting two new API models, GPT-Image-2.6 Flare and GPT-Image-2.6 Sunburst, designed for faster generation and higher-quality results, respectively. OpenAI has not yet published a standalone announcement detailing the new model's capabilities, and we could not independently test its performance.

The update follows the September release of ChatGPT Images 2.5, which introduced sharper details, more precise editing, and generation speeds up to 50% faster than its predecessor.

In our previous testing, Images 2.5 went head-to-head with Google's Nano Banana 2 across six categories, with each model winning three. OpenAI's model delivered particularly impressive illustration quality and improved on the oversharpening problems that affected earlier versions, although it still made mistakes when rendering text.

The move into GIF generation also comes months after OpenAI shut down Sora, its standalone AI video-generation app, in March. Animated GIFs are considerably simpler than the videos Sora generated, but the new feature brings a form of motion generation directly into ChatGPT without requiring a separate application.

OpenAI has not provided a specific date for when every ChatGPT account will receive GIF generation.

#4962. How Aussie Bitcoiners can avoid 2027’s shock CGT hike: Part 1

From setting up an SMSF or a company, to just leaving the country entirely, here are some strategies Australian Bitcoiners can employ to deal with 2027’s massive CGT hike.

The Australian government’s decision to scrap the 50% capital gains discount for all investments in the May budget came as a big shock for crypto owners. The new scheme effectively doubles the taxable component of a gain, minus a small discount for inflation.

But there are legal ways to minimize the amount of CGT you’ll have to pay — if you start prepping now.

Some Aussie crypto holders are taking drastic measures — like Finder founder and crypto king Fred Schebesta who is relocating to Singapore which offers 0% CGT, stating the tax changes were a “killer blow.”But there are less dramatic methods to reduce your CGT bills including setting up a self managed super fund (SMSF), a company to trade crypto at the company tax rate, or to just cross your fingers and hope the opposition will reverse the changes after the next election.

Since 1999, Aussie asset owners have only paid tax on half of their capital gain. From July 1, 2027 they’ll pay their full income tax rate — which means 30% CGT if your income and gains are between A$45,000 and $135,000, 37% for the portion between $135,000 and $190,000 and 47% above that. (All figures in this story are in Aussie dollars)

Adding insult to injury, low-income earners who aren’t on welfare like students and retirees will pay a minimum 30% CGT rate no matter how little the gain.

The only discount now available accounts for the effect of inflation on your cost basis. So if you buy a house for $1 million in 2027 and sell it for $1.5 million after 10 years, with inflation at 3%, you won’t have to pay tax on the $344,000 or so attributed to inflation.

“The challenge for cryptocurrency is that the CGT changes, indexation really helps slower growing assets not fast ones,” explains Andrea Yuen, Swyftx CEO.

So if you bought Bitcoin for $10,000 and sell it ten years later for $100,000, the inflation discount is only around $3,439. Under the old system you would pay tax on $45,000 of gains. Under the new system you’ll pay tax on $86,561 of gains.

Stockspot analyst Chris Bryki compared how ten different asset classes would fare under the new rules. “Crypto sits at the bottom because almost its entire return comes from capital growth,” he wrote in The Australian newspaper.

Here are a few different strategies to help crypto holders deal with the changes, ranging from little to no effort, to restructuring your entire life.

Any gain you’ve made before July 1 2027 will be taxed under the existing 50% discount scheme. So your best bet is to hope a raging bull market pushes the value of your portfolio as high as possible before that date.You should faithfully record the value of every crypto asset you have that day, as any gain from that value will be taxed at the higher rate.

Ben Simpson, founder of crypto advisory service Collective Shift, says Bitcoin’s performance since mid-year in a challenging macro environment bodes well for further gains before July.

But Simpson (who points out he’s not a financial advisor) adds, “I probably wouldn’t be betting the farm on the fact that we’re gonna have a huge bull run”

Yuen notes that prices aren’t within your control.  “A strategy isn’t a strategy if you’re just sort of hoping,” she says.

Both the official opposition The Coalition, and the insurgent opposition One Nation, have promised to reverse the changes. Crypto voters who feel strongly enough about this single issue, could vote for a change in government.However, the ruling ALP holds 94 out of 150 seats in the House of Representatives and will be difficult to dislodge from office. If the government does change — and the legislation can pass the Senate in time — it’s technically possible the changes could be reversed before anyone has to file taxes from July 1 2028.

But it’s probably more likely that any change to the system would occur after that date. Adrian Forza, director of Crypto Tax Australia, says this would make calculating gains very difficult.

Australian retirement funds are called superannuation funds, and once you are over 60 you can access your investments tax-free — even as they keep growing.Unfortunately none of the retail super funds allow clients to invest in crypto, so if you want to protect your crypto gains using this method, you’d have to set up an SMSF. About a third of Simpson’s high net worth clients invest in crypto via SMSFs.  “Buying Bitcoin and accumulating it there is a super solid idea,” he says, although he cautions against adding many altcoins. “You’re risking your retirement, which is an ultra long-term hold, on short-term altcoins,” he says.

Australian super fund AMP has invested in Bitcoin — but doesn’t allow its clients to do so.

The big drawback with SMSFs — explains the 27-year-old founder — is that younger crypto investors can’t access the money for decades.

Realistically then, it’s a strategy more suited to wealthier investors aged over 50, who have a shorter period to wait.

Related: Older investors are risking everything for a crypto-funded retirement

The government has also made running an SMSF a pain in the backside, with lots of compliance paperwork and audits, as well as a bunch of unavoidable fees that make it prohibitive for smaller balances. Annual costs typically range between $2,000 and $4,000, although online providers like Stake can reduce total annual costs to around $1,330, as long as you only buy Bitcoin or Ether ETFs.

You also have to sell any crypto and pay tax on it before you can rebuy it with your SMSF, and there are annual contribution limits to factor in, although Forza says you should be able to get almost $400,000 in there with a bit of creativity.

Keep an eye out in December for a detailed report on how to set up a crypto SMSF.

A strategy some billionaires employ to avoid paying tax is by borrowing against their assets, as repaying the loan often works out cheaper than paying tax on a sale.

Bitcoiners can now access a range of lenders, including BlockEarner, Veild and Swyftx, that let you employ a similar strategy by borrowing against your BTC as collateral.

“We like to bring that strategy to the masses, not just to billionaires,” laughs Yuen.

But it can be risky if you don’t have a billionaire’s deep pockets. Borrowers face high interest rates, relatively short loan periods (typically 12 months), and risk losing their Bitcoin if the market moves against them. You can’t borrow against the full value either — Swyftx has a loan-to-value ratio around 40%.”If your collateral drops below a certain amount, there will be a margin call,” she says.  “I would say it’s a bet on a price going up. I think you have to have a view that crypto will go up — or at least not go down so that you get margin-called... Otherwise, a crypto-backed loan wouldn’t make sense for you.”

Simpson doesn’t recommend this strategy due to the risks, but says it can pay off for some people. For example if you borrow at 10% interest, and Bitcoin compounds by 33% a year (as it has done in the past) you’d end up much better off than paying CGT.

But he warns it’s a leveraged bet that is best suited to sophisticated investors.

Bitcoin is down 28% over the past year, so anyone borrowing a year ago would have faced margin calls and possible liquidation as well as interest costs.

Borrowing against Bitcoin for a house deposit has become a more popular strategy. (BlockEarner)

Forza cautions that borrowing crypto can also be a CGT event in some circumstances, such as borrowing in DeFi. If you deposit ETH to Aave, it gets automatically swapped to WETH which  is a taxable event, and when you withdraw the ETH it’s another taxable event.

Borrowing from centralized lenders like BlockEarner or Swyftx is currently a grey area, and the ATO has not clarified if it’s a taxable event.”If you’re talking about [borrowing] ten grand fine. But if you’ve got three to five Bitcoin, I wouldn’t risk it because if it goes against you then you’re going to be paying tax.””Vield is the only one in Australia that’s got a private ruling that says their product is not a taxable event. No one else has got one.”Swftxy confirmed it has not got a private ruling and said clients should seek tax advice, while BlockEarner did not respond to a request for comment.

In part two of our Aussie CGT special, coming later this week, we’ll bring you the final four strategies to minimize your crypto tax bills, including setting up a company or leaving the country entirely.

#4959. Redwood and Rivian set to expand their EV battery recycling partnership

Redwood Materials and Rivian are expanding their existing EV battery partnership, adding a fresh battery material recycling effort to the EV startup’s Normal, Illinois, vehicle factory and, potentially, other Rivian-owned sites.

As Tesla co-founder JB Straubel’s second act, Redwood Materials had a lot to live up to. Its plan to give used EV batteries a second life as energy storage for public- and private-sector buildings, construction sites, micro-grids, and (sigh) data centers makes plenty of sense, though. Enough sense, anyway, that Rivian decided to put the idea to work as it built out its R2 production line at the former Diamond-Star Mitsubishi plant in Normal. Back in April, the upstart EV brand signed on for 10 MWh worth of energy storage using repurposed Rivian battery packs – but that deal was just the beginning.

The new, expanded partnership between the two companies will see Redwood collect, sort, and recycle Rivian’s battery production scrap materials, putting them safely back into the manufacturing process with minimal waste and at a fraction of the cost of virgin material. What’s more, the new deal will see retired Rivian batteries put to use in other stationary energy projects before eventually recycling some 99% of those batteries’ critical materials (nickel, cobalt, copper, and lithium).

With the recent launch of what is sure to be the more popular, more affordable R2, that’s suddenly a much bigger deal than it might have been last year.

“Every Rivian battery pack that comes back to us is a potential source of domestic grid energy storage in addition to critical materials,” explains Straubel. “Redwood’s unique ability to integrate and redeploy Rivian batteries with our technology is an opportunity to enable fast, flexible, and more cost-efficient energy storage. As the demand and cost for energy keep rising, working with companies like Rivian is how we help meet these challenges.”

Rivian is the latest major EV brand to enter into a formal battery recycling partnership with Redwood, with the list of others including BMW, Ford, Toyota, and Volvo.

#4957. Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Four flaws in Bitcoin’s consensus rules that have lingered for years.

Issues like quantum resistance may command attention, but a practical cleanup is what many experts think Bitcoin’s next soft fork is likely to address, spanning fixes for vulnerabilities in the network’s difficulty adjustment mechanism to denial-of-service vectors among miners.

Proposed in 2019 and known as the Great Consensus Cleanup (BIP-54), the upgrade seeks to address the “cruft” in Bitcoin’s consensus design through changes that have historically been sidelined, as described by long-time Bitcoin developer and BIP-54 co-author Matt Corallo.

“There’s a laundry list of things we know of that we really need to clean up,” he said during a recent appearance on MARA Foundation TV. “All of these issues are real concerns that could impact Bitcoin negatively, but they’re not like, ‘Oh, the sky is falling.’”

Although a formal timeline for the BIP’s undertaking hasn’t been established, Corallo thinks there’s an 80% chance that the Great Consensus Cleanup is up next for the network. During his talk with Head of MARA Foundation Isabel Foxen Duke, he also called it a practical exercise for Bitcoin developers who haven’t yet been through a soft fork.

At its core, Corallo said BIP-54 “fixes some stuff that everyone agrees are problems,” while targeting four specific vulnerabilities:

Still, not everyone views a maintenance-focused soft fork as a major win. On X, Paul Sztorc, co-founder and CEO of LayerTwo Labs, called BIP-54’s potential activation “kind of depressing, since it is a janitorial maintenance fork that doesn't do anything.”

Surveying developers at TABconf in Atlanta, Sztorc noted that Bitcoin’s “best and brightest” predominantly expect the Great Consensus Cleanup to be Bitcoin’s next soft fork anyway, with the earliest estimate in September 2027, and most pointing to 2028 or later.

Following the activation of Taproot in 2021, Bitcoin’s community has faced years of gridlock surrounding protocol changes. This friction is frequently framed around “ossification,” with broader protocol changes like OP_CAT (BIP-347) and OP_CHECKTEMPLATEVERIFY (BIP-119) struggling to reach ecosystem-wide consensus.

According to Corallo, soft forks have always involved overcoming technical hurdles. But more recently, he’s observed political and social friction that’s become just as relevant.

The Bitcoin community spent months debating how Taproot should be activated, with arguments pitting miner-signaling thresholds against mandatory user flag days. Corallo argues that BIP-54 can establish a deployment playbook for future soft forks.

“In every recent soft fork, there’s also been a bunch of drama about activation mechanisms,” he said. “So hopefully we could do a soft fork to go through that drama, and then copy and paste and reuse it and head off some of that drama [for future soft forks].”

In that sense, BIP-54 is just as much about proving Bitcoin can still agree on upgrades at the social layer as it is about actual code cleanup.

André Beganski is a Senior Content Manager at MARA Foundation, producing content on topics including the intersection of quantum computing and cryptography. He has previously worked for Decrypt.

#4953. Crypto PACs eye 2028 ‘new map’ as Democrats take Congress

Crypto super PACs spent more than $176 million on the midterms, but the Democratic sweep raises the prospect of tougher industry scrutiny.

Two years after crypto money helped drive Sherrod Brown out of the Senate, roughly $64 million couldn’t keep him from returning.

Brown’s victory over Republican Jon Husted in Ohio delivered the industry its biggest electoral loss of the 2026 midterms as Democrats won control of both chambers of Congress. The sweep also puts Sen. Elizabeth Warren, one of crypto’s fiercest critics, in line to chair the Senate Banking Committee.

Crypto super PACs spent about $176 million across 100 seats during the election cycle, including primaries, according to Reuters, up from more than $130 million in 2024. Fairshake and its affiliated PACs accounted for $154.5 million across 89 races.

“The next Congress will bring a fundamentally different environment for crypto in Washington, and the industry should expect significantly more scrutiny,” Kyle Bligen, executive director at the Decentralization Research Center, told Cointelegraph.

Fairshake, meanwhile, is left with more than $67 million heading to the 2028 elections. “Newly elected and current members will have a choice: protect consumers and American jobs or fight progress,” spokesperson Geoff Vetter told Cointelegraph in an email. “We will be watching and ‘28 is a new map.”

Ohio was the industry’s biggest bet as crypto PACs put more than $64 million into keeping Senator Jon Husted in office. His opponent, former Sen. Sherrod Brown, won the special election to complete former Sen. JD Vance’s term, which ends in January 2029. He could seek a senior role on the Senate Agriculture Committee, which oversees the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC).

Brown’s comeback came two years after crypto’s biggest win in 2024, when Fairshake spent $40 million to help Bernie Moreno unseat him in Ohio’s other Senate seat. Some industry leaders treated that result as a warning to Congress. Gemini exchange co-founder Cameron Winklevoss declared on X: “This is what happens when you mess with the crypto army”.

This year, crypto spending was part of a much larger flood of outside money. Independent expenditures in the race topped $350 million, while Brown turned the crypto spending into a campaign theme, writing “I’ll be damned if crypto billionaires decide this race,” in a campaign message.

The loss in Ohio may weaken the perception that defying crypto PACs carries an electoral cost in Washington. Before the vote, a Republican crypto lobbyist told Politico that a Brown win would “neuter Fairshake’s perceived Sword of Damocles.”

Nebraska, meanwhile, offered crypto PACs a significant win, with Sen. Pete Ricketts retaining his seat after Fairshake affiliate Defend American Jobs spent $13.5 million opposing independent challenger Dan Osborn.

“As the biggest spender in the Nebraska Senate race, we’re proud to have helped Sen. Ricketts weather the waves of a hard-fought cycle,” wrote Vetter. “Fairshake played a meaningful role in winning competitive races across the country in 2026 and we will do it again in 2028.” The PAC’s other Senate wins came in Republican strongholds, including Barry Moore in Alabama and Andy Barr in Kentucky.

Other spending failed to deliver the intended results, both in primaries and in November. The Fairshake network’s roughly $10 million spent opposing Juliana Stratton in the Illinois Democratic primary failed to block her nomination. She later won the Senate seat. In Massachusetts, John Deaton lost despite roughly $700,000 in spending by crypto-backed Commonwealth Unity Fund to help elect him. Ken Paxton, who received crypto PAC backing in his successful Texas Republican runoff against John Cornyn, went on to lose to James Talarico.

The House brought another setback when digital assets subcommittee chair Bryan Steil lost to Democrat Mitchell Berman despite $1.9 million in crypto PAC funding.

The shift in congressional control could slow crypto legislation, but BitGo CEO and co-founder Mike Belshe said the election results would not alter the industry’s trajectory. “Crypto has continued to move forward under different administrations and different political majorities. The GENIUS Act is now law, stablecoin rules are moving ahead, and regulators are continuing to work on tokenization and digital asset markets.”Democratic victories could also bring crypto supporters into the Senate. Advocacy group Stand With Crypto counted 19 winning Senate candidates it classified as pro-crypto and eight as anti-crypto, with 33 of the 35 Senate races called as of 9:45 p.m. UTC on Nov. 4.

Despite the crypto PAC spending against him in Texas, Talarico voted for the Texas Bitcoin reserve as a state lawmaker and is rated by the group as strongly supportive of crypto.

For industry advocates, the next challenge is building relationships with incoming lawmakers. Jessica Martinez, US policy director at Fireblocks, told Cointelegraph that many newly elected members had limited records on crypto, making “educating members early” key to shaping the Democratic policy agenda.

#4951. The 2027 Ford Mustang Mach-E gains a standard long-range battery and starts at $40,000

Ford opened orders for the 2027 Mustang Mach-E, which now offers a standard extended-range battery for under $40,000. A new Trail model is also joining the lineup.

Ford’s electric crossover SUV is back for another model year. The 2027 Mustang Mach-E drops the standard-range battery offered in the 2026 model and now comes standard with the larger 88 kWh battery.

The 2027 Ford Mustang Mach-e is available in five trims: Select, Premium, GT, Rally, and a new purpose-built Trail model.

The new 2027 Ford Mustang Mach-E Trail is available for a $2,295 package upgrade for the Select eAWD.

According to Ford, the new Trail package is designed to make “outdoor adventures across pavement, gravel, and dirt roads more accessible than ever.”

It features fine-tuned front and rear struts and springs, a Rally-shaped steering rack, 19-inch High Gloss Dark Carbonized Gray Wheels with as-cast graphics, and is 10 mm taller in the front and 15 mm taller in the rear than the base Mach-E.

The 2027 Mustang Mach-E Trail comes with standard 400W, 120V Pro Power Onboard. It’s available as an upgrade for other variants, including the Premium, GT, and Mustang Mach-E Rally.

Select and Premium models use the 88 kWh battery and are available with rear- or all-wheel-drive (eAWD) powertrains. The GT and Rally variants use the 91 kWh battery and are only sold as eAWD models.

Ford’s BlueCruise 1.5 comes standard on the GT and Rally and optional on Select with Tech Pack Lite and Premium with Premium Tech Pack models. The Trail requires the hardware selection at the time of ordering. It cannot be added later.

Meanwhile, the Ford Connectivity Package and Ford Security Package are standard across the entire 2027 lineup.

The base 2027 Ford Mustang Mach-E Select RWD starts at $39,995. Including the $2,045 destination fee, prices start at $42,040.

While it is a bit more expensive than the 2026 model year, Ford no longer offers the standard-range battery. Ford said a new secondary front motor delivers the same performance as the previous unit but at a lower cost, enabling the lower-priced base RWD model with the extended-range battery.

We're at almost 80,000 miles in our Mach E GT and we'll drive it to 200,000+. It's a blast to drive and we've loved owning it. Feels as solid now as it did day 1.

Bought a set of summer tires at 43k miles. Other than that, and swapping between winter/summer tires, the maintenance cost has been $0.

We don't DC fast charge very often, so the slower L3 charging isn't a big deal for us - but does need improvement IMO.

Previously, the extended-range battery was available, starting at $45,345 on the 2026 Mustang Mach-E.

The extended-range (88 kWh) battery provides an EPA-estimated driving range of up to 320 miles in RWD models. Depending on the configuration, eAWD models offer 300 miles of range. The GT and Rally use the 91 kWh battery, providing 280 miles and 255 miles of range, respectively.

Ford will announce the final EPA-estimated driving range for the Trail model closer to when it goes on sale, expected in the first quarter of 2027.

#4950. How This New Orleans Hotel Went From Former Haunted House to Chic Destination

The Greek Revival mansion that sits at the corner of Prytania Street and Jackson Avenue in New Orleans’ Garden District wouldn’t know “dull” if it came up and rang the doorbell. The 1857 building’s first owner was a cotton merchant who died of yellow fever. Another owner was fatally struck by lightning. Fast-forward to the 21st century, when a Tina Turner impersonator bought the property and turned it into Magnolia Mansion, a B&B with suites with names like Vampire Lovers’ Lair and Moulin Rouge. A former professional boxer and local politics kingmaker took over, but things fell apart around the time of the FBI’s raid on the property.

Oh, and the estate is said to be haunted.

The Whit's moody mahogany-paneled library

Named in its B&B era by USA Today as one of the top places in the nation “to sleep with a ghost” the magisterial Harris-Maginnis House earned a spot on the city’s ghost-tour circuit with an ever-mounting pile of lore. According to a spooky tour-operator website, guests at the B&B reported various eerie happenings over the years, such as the sensation of somebody tucking them in at night or a presence watching them while they slept. There were also rumblings of mysterious footsteps and slamming doors. Ghosts or no ghosts, the new team taking over the place found gothic-coded wall murals and dark purple velvet hanging at every turn.

Who you gonna call to give an old haunt a new design vernacular? Gretchen Trauth, CEO of the New Orleans investment firm behind the New Orleans hotel, rang up Jensen Logan and Katie Leblanc Killen of Logan Killen, a local design firm known for infusing spaces with a beguiling mix of polish and patina. “The goal was a hotel where nobody can quite tell whether a designer has touched it,” says Logan.

Breakfast room with Pierre Frey wallpaper

“We wanted it to feel very storied, like it’s been someone’s house for a long time,” adds Killen. “There are these layered histories imbued in the furnishings and the art and accessories.”

Named for poet Walt Whitman, who spent three months in New Orleans around the time of the home’s construction, the designers have reimagined the property with a romantic, lived-in, and curio-studded aesthetic that tempts guests to sit down and stay awhile. Architect Seamus McGuire of New Orleans firm Cicada helped navigate historical codes and redraw the map to capture some of the glamour that had drained away in recent years. His blueprint called for converting a trio of bedrooms into lounges. A mahogany-paneled library is now a gallery of antique dog portraits and a repository of volumes from local independent Faulkner House Books. “It’s dark and moody, and where you might want to have a cocktail or a whiskey,” Logan says of the clubby room. Bookworms might also gravitate to the sunroom’s banquette seating, one of the wicker chairs on the wraparound porch, or the breakfast room, a delightfully matchy-matchy enclave covered in Pierre Frey Les Colonnes wallpaper and curtains.

The hardest room to get right, say the designers, was the main lounge. “It’s a giant space that isn’t always in use, and can be tricky to give life to when it’s empty,” says Killen. Intended to serve as a great room by day and an atmospheric cocktail bar come evening, the team layered in elements such as blue paint that reads ethereal by day and warm by night, a custom grid carpet to add a sense of tailoring to the expansive area, and an intimate salon-style wall of works by Louisiana artists, including Robert Moreland and Mallory Page.

Another tightrope walk was the second-floor bar. Landmark rules forbid faking history a space never had, so the team went openly Deco instead. “What we don’t want is to put a contemporary bar into a very historic property,” says McGuire. “You can’t give a false sense of history to a space.”

It will probably come as little surprise that the bedrooms resist uniformity. In addition to the classically designed units, a quartet of cozy garden rooms has low ceilings and glossy oxblood walls; three cabana rooms feel airy, with bamboo beds and high ceilings. “It’s not meant to feel like everything was changed by the same person at the same time,” Killen says of the narrative that she and Logan have introduced to the New Orleans hotel.

As for the supposed ghosts, nobody’s heard boo.

#4947. Tesla (TSLA) quietly cuts 2026 capex guidance to $20B

Tesla has lowered its 2026 capital spending forecast. In its Q3 10-Q, the company now says it expects capital expenditures “to be in excess of $20 billion in 2026,” down from the “more than $25 billion” it had guided since April.

The change was in the 10-Q Tesla filed Wednesday, not in the shareholder letter it released the same day. And it lands in a quarter where Tesla reported $1.53 billion of positive free cash flow, a number that depends a lot on timing.

Tesla started 2026 guiding to about $20 billion in capex. In April, it raised that to more than $25 billion, and in July, CFO Vaibhav Taneja reiterated the figure while Elon Musk called 2026 a “massive capex year.”

Now it’s back to “in excess of $20 billion.”

Tesla has spent $13.01 billion so far this year: $2.49 billion in Q1, $5.79 billion in Q2 and $4.73 billion in Q3. Hitting $25 billion would have required about $12 billion in Q4 alone, more than double its biggest quarter. The new floor only requires about $7 billion.

The 10-Q says 2026 spending is “driven by our SI initiatives, including investments in compute infrastructure and data centers,” along with factory ramps and Tesla’s fleet of “company-operated SI-enabled assets.”

There’s a catch in the cash numbers, though. Tesla had $3.67 billion of equipment and construction spending sitting in liabilities at the end of September, meaning it was booked but not yet paid. That’s up from $2.63 billion at the end of June and $1.63 billion a year ago. So Tesla’s actual spending is running ahead of the cash capex it reports.

Tesla reported $6.26 billion in operating cash flow and $1.53 billion in free cash flow after capex. Here’s how it got there:

Depreciation and stock compensation are normal add-backs. They reduce profit without using cash.

The working capital line is the one to watch. Without that $2.59 billion, Tesla’s free cash flow would have been about negative $1.06 billion.

Most of it came from bills Tesla hasn’t paid yet. Accounts payable rose $1.92 billion in the quarter and accrued liabilities rose $1.95 billion, including $722 million more in “accrued purchases,” goods Tesla received but hadn’t been invoiced for.

Another $1.26 billion came from deferred revenue, mostly energy storage. Tesla’s energy deferred revenue jumped to $3.30 billion from $2.31 billion. But not all of that is cash. Tesla disclosed that it billed SpaceX $588 million for Megapacks “that have not been delivered,” which SpaceX hadn’t paid at quarter-end. That amount sits in both deferred revenue and accounts receivable, which rose $565 million.

Those gains were partly offset by a $1.82 billion increase in prepaid expenses and other assets, and a small inventory build.

If you also count the $1.03 billion increase in unpaid capex, Tesla’s free cash flow on an “as spent” basis was closer to $500 million.

The capex cut comes a month after Tesla signed $30 billion of new credit facilities. The 10-Q details them: a $20 billion delayed-draw term loan maturing in 2029, and $10 billion of revolving credit replacing a $5 billion facility. Tesla hadn’t drawn on either at quarter-end, and it had $45.4 billion in cash and investments.

Rearranging the deck chairs ahead of the merger?

None of this is improper in today’s accounting standards, which we can argue are pretty awful. Paying suppliers later, collecting deposits, and booking capex when it’s incurred are all somewhat normal accounting, and Tesla disclosed them. But they explain why the “$1.5 billion of free cash flow” headline from Wednesday’s results says less about the business than it seems.

Underneath, Tesla made $718 million of operating income, less than its $1.17 billion in stock compensation, and before working capital, it burned cash. The timing items that helped this quarter will reverse when the invoices come due and the Megapacks ship.

The capex cut is the more interesting signal. Six months ago, Tesla was telling investors 2026 would be a massive spending year to build out AI compute, Optimus and Cybercab. Now it’s lowering the target in its 10-Q instead of the shareholder letter, while lining up $30 billion of debt. Either projects are slipping into 2027, or Tesla is spending less to protect its cash. Tesla should say which.

If you’re paying attention to what EVs cost to own, home solar is how you get the “fuel” side close to zero. With electricity rates up almost 10% last year and expected to keep climbing, going solar is one of the best ways to protect yourself against rising costs. #4945. This Shell-Shaped Villa Has Mediterranean Views and a Windswept Feel

When designer Federica Gosio talks about this project in Sardinia, she describes it as “an arch over the sea.” Gosio—who leads the firm bearing her name and is also a cofounder of Studio MILO—explains, “the truly unique aspect of this home is its ability to adapt to its surroundings, the sea, and nature.” Overlooking the bay of Porto Rafael, an area on the island’s northern coast famous for its remote location and its scenic beauty, the house resembles a seashell.

The villa looks like a white shell that has emerged from the sea to embrace the surrounding landscape .

There is no single guiding principle in Gosio’s design; instead, the shell-shaped villa embodies a capacity to adapt: “The aim is to give shape to ever-changing realities, integrating it with strength and grace into a larger context,” she says. It’s an approach that characterizes many of Gosio’s projects. This one springs from the rugged land where the home is located and pays homage to its setting through its design.

A custom-made bench follows the perimeter of the portico, wrapping around the space. At its center is a set of furnishings in wood and neutral-toned fabrics.

Working with Gosio, Alessandra Radaelli, the owner of this shell-shaped villa, created an organic, sculptural retreat. “We decided to honor the design legacy of Rafael Neville,” she says, referring to the founder of Porto Rafael. That meant “favoring a harmonious approach in which architecture blends with the landscape.”

Interior designer Federica Gosio on the terrace of this unique and intriguing villa.

The villa is characterized by curved lines and pure white surfaces that also have a textured quality. Rattan and straw accents add warmth to the space.

The home’s pointed arches, organic geometries, and natural materials were inspired by the earth and the sea. “The minimalist spaces were designed to embrace the sunlight, the wind, and the slow rhythm of island life,” Gosio explains. “It’s a project that interprets the Mediterranean in a contemporary way through a refined balance of memory, nature, and design.”

Every corner of this villa blends seamlessly with its surroundings: In this outdoor space, the shapes soften and the lines curve. At the center is a white rattan lounge set.

With its soft curves, the home feels as if it has been shaped by the winds that blow across Sardinia. During the winter, the Mistral here is powerful, sweeping across the island and reshaping the landscape. It looks almost as if the same process has taken place both inside and outside the villa. The reality is that this effect was a deliberate design choice to soften the edges of the villa and smooth out its different volumes.

This semicircular arch draws one’s gaze to the sea. In the foreground are two coffee tables by Mogg. In the background, on a terrace, rattan armchairs complement the organic curves of the seaside architecture.

Both inside and out, the villa is a whitewashed structure that follows the contours of the land. Gosio and her team have done away with straight lines, opting instead for a softer silhouette. The signature element of the home is undoubtedly the pointed arch, used for both the villa’s doors and its hallways. “The soft volumes, textured surfaces, and carefully placed rocks guide the visitor toward the entrance with its hand-brushed, iron arched door, custom-designed by Daniele Mingardo,” Gosio says.

The property’s garden is an idyllic green expanse leading down to the sea with junipers and various plants native to the Mediterranean framing the villa.

Interior designer Federica Gosio (on the left) and homeowner Alessandra Radaelli.

Surrounding the home are acres of land covered in lush Mediterranean vegetation leading down to the sea, with the route including green terraces and social spaces, including the panoramic pool and an outdoor kitchen. The feeling is that of an earthly Eden planted in a corner of Sardinia.

The organic arch reveals a living space featuring an Asawa sofa by Secolo, Dune side tables by Mogg, and a vintage rattan pendant lamp purchased on the Pamono website. The Paysage II rug is by Atelier Février, the blue ceramic Anfora Anulare vase is by Walter Usai, and the cups and saucers are by LR Ceramics.

The goal of respecting and celebrating the villa’s setting is also reflected in the colors and materials used throughout the home. “The white walls, the floors in Orosei marble with a bush hammered finish and ‘broken edges,’ and the organically shaped furnishings come together to create a measured and well-proportioned design, inspired by the neutral and warm tones of the soil here,” Gosio says.

Access to the kitchen is also through a large, sweeping arch. The custom oak cabinets are paired with WA ceramic tiles by Decoratori Bassanesi on the wall. The table, which resembles a sculpture, is the Luna Elíptica by La Nena.

Orosei stone, which is found on the coast of Sardinia, not only forms the floors but also the sinks (both indoor and outdoor), a clear example of how the island was integrated into the design. Rounding out the composition is a selection of vintage rattan pieces and sculptural creations by Pretziada, a creative design practice based in Sardinia, and its artisans. Nothing is left to chance and every element contributes to the home’s exquisite design and its appealing composition.

At the center of the dining area, which overlooks the outdoor space, is a Luna Elíptica table by La Nena. Next to it is a Lia Chair by Chiara Andreatti and Pierpaolo Mandis for Pretziada, a piece that is both handcrafted and contemporary, with roots in traditional Sardinian design. The Holden Linear Pendant Uplight is from Porta Romana. On the table are several ceramic pieces by LR Ceramics.

The infinity-edge pool blends seamlessly into the villa’s architecture. Alongside it is a sundeck surrounded by lush green foliage and aromatic plants.

The outdoor spaces in this villa are extensions of its interior. Wooden furnishings with a distinct nautical flair are set amid breathtaking green spaces with ocean views. On the table is a Mini Geen-A lamp by Kartell.

A custom outdoor kitchen with views of the sea. The sink is made of Orosei marble.

The pool has views of the sea and sunsets.

The primary bedroom, with its organic lines, overlooks the pool and, beyond it, the sea. The custom headboard is upholstered in a striped fabric. Next to it is a Holden Wall Light from Porta Romana.

In the guest room, the custom headboard sets the tone. Next to it is a pendant lamp with a rattan Braided Lampshade-Bottle by Ferm Living. Atop a stone nightstand, a vase by LR Ceramics.

In this bath, Totem tiles from the Nok collection by 41zero42 add texture to the wall. The sink is by Catalano, and the faucets are from the Sirius series by Rubinetterie Stella.

This bath, with its rich textural quality, is defined by the wall-mounted sink in Orosei stone paired with a Roma faucet set by Rubinetterie Stella.

This room evokes traditional Sardinian design with a custom headboard that’s a mosaic of shapes and neutral tones, upholstered in Zinc’s Cubilete Natural fabric. The Holden Wall Light is from Porta Romana, as is the Huxley Jordan Wall Light reading lamp.

#4943. Fortescue is using KUKA welding robots to handle 6 MW charging cables [video]

Humanoid robots aren’t ready to juice up the massive, 240-tonne electric haul trucks Fortescue is putting to work in Australia’s punishing Pilbara. Instead, the company is using specially modified and massively powerful automotive assembly-line robots from KUKA to wrangle the massive cables needed to deliver a mind-bending 6 MW of power to its trucks’ batteries.

While Chinese car brands capable of 2 MW “flash charging” are making headlines these days, Fortescue and Liebherr have been quietly working together to develop a 6MW charging system for their massive, ultra heavy-duty mining rigs since 2024 – but details about how the process actually works have been scarce.

Until now, that is. In a new video released by the company earlier this month, Fortescue explains how it delivers what amounts a controlled lightning bolt into the battery of its specially developed, 264 (short) ton Liebherr T264E electric haul truck. And, because the massive, liquid-cooled copper cables are too big, too heavy, and too dangerous for any human to handle, of course they’re using robots to get the job done.

“The challenge with a six megawatt charger is that nobody makes these, and therefore nobody makes a lot of the components, either,” says a Fortescue representative, in the video. “So, we’ve had to take systems and subsystems from different industries were using industrial robots that would normally be used for welding in a car factory.”

Fortescue was able to develop and refine its KUKA robots with help from a $10 million (AUD) grant from the Australian Renewable Energy Agency (ARENA), which were earmarked to help the company develop a six megawatt (MW) fast charger test unit and support its ultimate goal of achieving true net zero operations by 2030.

“Leveraging our world-class capability in battery and charging solutions from motorsport, the fast chargers have been developed for the challenging conditions of the Pilbara,” explained Fortescue Zero’s then-Chief Executive Officer, Ellie Coates, when the grant was announced in 2024. “Equipped with robotic connection options, they will be able to power Fortescue’s future 240-tonne Liebherr T 264 battery electric trucks in just 30 minutes.”

No need to reinvent the wheel, Cruise ship and other large vessels have ben using shore power for years when docked. these typical range from 7 MW to 16 MW, just borrow their tech, a overhead crane to lift the cable and an operator to plug it on

The Liebherr T264E packs an impossibly large 3.2 MWh (3,200 kWh) battery pack – the equivalent capacity of about 40 road-going Tesla Model Y crossovers – to put that 30 minute spec into context. You can check out the company’s official video, below, then let us know what you think of the 6 MW charging robots in the comments.

#4942. BitMart founder says security team ‘severely understaffed’ after new hack

The crypto exchange’s founder warned of security staffing shortages amid restructuring as analysts flagged suspicious transfers potentially linked to the Mercado Bitcoin hack.

Cryptocurrency exchange BitMart is operating with an understaffed security team following restructuring, its founder said Wednesday, after confirming a new hack that may be linked to the recent attack on Brazil’s exchange Mercado Bitcoin.

In a post on X, BitMart founder Sheldon Xia said threats against employees and alleged retaliation by former staff had complicated efforts to retain technical personnel.

“It took considerable effort and individual conversations to persuade some key technical staff to remain and maintain our core systems,” Xia wrote. “However, we continue to face numerous attacks. Our security team is currently severely understaffed, but we are still doing everything we can to safeguard data and assets.”

Xia described the amount involved in Monday’s hack as small but did not disclose a figure. Blockchain analytics firm Bitquery identified approximately $391,000 in suspicious transfers from wallets mostly associated with BitMart across three blockchains, according to a Wednesday investigation.

Bitquery traced the funds to a wallet also implicated in the more than $46 million Mercado Bitcoin hack on Monday, Nov. 2, supporting Xia’s preliminary suspicion of a possible connection between the incidents.

Related: North Korea denies role in $6.3B Binance hack, points finger at US

The latest breach comes amid months of uncertainty over BitMart’s future. The exchange announced in July that it would end trading on Aug. 26 and cease operations in January 2027. Following the announcement, users reported delayed withdrawals and frozen accounts, raising concerns about access to their funds.

In August, BitMart’s Chinese-language X account alleged that employees had gone unpaid and demanded answers about customer funds. The founder dismissed the allegations as fabricated, though it remained unclear who controlled the account at the time.

In a restructuring proposal published Monday, the exchange outlined three potential recovery options for users, including liquid asset distributions and recovery-linked tokens, with plans to seek court approval in December or January.

Cointelegraph contacted BitMart for details about the restructuring, security staffing and latest breach but had not received a response by publication.

Separately, a crypto crime-fighting alliance formed by Tron, Tether and TRM Labs has dissolved amid a wave of major industry hacks, Bloomberg reported Wednesday.

The T3 Financial Crime Unit, launched in September 2024 and later joined by Binance, struggled with slow responses and poor coordination, Bloomberg reported, citing people familiar with the matter. One source said the group disbanded in late August.

In May, T3 said it had helped freeze more than $450 million in assets linked to suspected criminal activity since its launch in 2024.

The group’s dissolution comes amid a wave of major crypto hacks, including last week’s $6.3 billion Binance breach, the largest cryptocurrency theft on record.
